4:T8b1,## What (admin action, exact)

As repo admin, enable branch protection (or equivalent rulesets) on **`develop` AND `main`** requiring these 9 status checks — all of which now exist and run green at develop tip:

1. `All Tests Passed`
2. `Develop Gate (lint)`
3. `Develop Gate (secret scan + UI determinism)`
4. `Format + Type Safety Ratchet`
5. `Homepage Build (PR smoke)`
6. `gitleaks`
7. `coverage on changed files`
8. `check-pr-title`
9. `stale-base guard`

Plus (recommended): require a PR before merging, block force-pushes, and keep admins-can-bypass off for `main`.

## Current live state (verified 2026-07-04)

```
gh api repos/elizaOS/eliza/branches/develop --jq .protected  → false
gh api repos/elizaOS/eliza/branches/main --jq .protected     → false
gh api repos/elizaOS/eliza/rules/branches/develop            → []
gh api repos/elizaOS/eliza/rules/branches/main               → []
```

Both branches are fully unprotected: any push-access account (or agent) can merge a red PR or push directly, and every check above is advisory-only.

## Why the agent fleet cannot self-serve

NubsCarson fleet permission on this repo is `push: true, admin: false` (verified via `gh api repos/elizaOS/eliza --jq .permissions`). Branch protection / rulesets are repo-admin-only API surfaces — the PUT returns 403 for us. Only @lalalune (org admin) can flip this.

## What it unblocks

- The entire #11853 lane investment (all 9 lanes made green) becomes *enforced* instead of advisory — this is the explicitly deferred "Enforcement flip (step 2)" from #11853's closeout.
- Blocks the #11271 class of incident (stale-base mass-revert reached develop; the `stale-base guard` check exists but is unenforced today).
- Blocks red merges to `main` ahead of prod deploys.

## Relationship to #12719 (merge queue)

#12719 is the richer end-state for `develop` (merge-queue ruleset + single aggregate check). If shaw prefers to land #12719 directly, that supersedes the `develop` half of this issue — but `main` protection is needed either way, and this issue is the 5-minute fallback that stops red merges **now** without waiting on the merge-queue workflow prerequisite.

cc @lalalune

Observed on PR #16344, run 29322794026 / job 87051850019:

```
RuntimeError: git could not determine source provenance:
fatal: detected dubious ownership in repository at /workspace/repo
```

The failure is unrelated to the two `.gitignore` changes in that PR and follows from the current `develop` workflow/container contract.

- Keep the trust exception scoped to `/workspace/repo`; do not disable ownership checks globally.

The packaged OCR triage can classify a fully rendered launcher as `broken` / "pixels are blank" even when the authoritative DOM/pixel audit reports a healthy populated view. This is a distinct residual split from #15912 before that inbox settle-race issue closes.

Two unrelated, same-day PR runs reproduced the exact result for `builtin-rolodex @ mobile-portrait`:

- PR #15886: [run 29063969087](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/29063969087), OCR artifact `8216887389`, ZIP digest `sha256:bc770555b8e593b15008ad35d50da237cc35c813ac739e636e06126ffb841aef`.
- PR #15900: [run 29063521666](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/29063521666), OCR artifact `8216703067`, ZIP digest `sha256:1cbe4db9fd7d0d7bbe275865487efc6eafd59c0d413d47f6a6d018310fbe4597`.

Both `ocr-triage.json` files contain the same false verdict:

```json
{
  "slug": "builtin-rolodex",
  "viewport": "mobile-portrait",
  "domVerdict": "good",
  "ocrVerdict": "broken",
  "reasons": ["pixels are blank — view painted no readable text"],
  "regression": true,
  "text": "oY)"
}
```

The corresponding real capture is not blank. I downloaded and manually opened `mobile-portrait/builtin-rolodex.png` from aesthetic artifact `8216843748` (ZIP digest `sha256:be6d86dc9174fe422f64ce64f0a79be1a78625aed2cef55165d1a3bbd5e27d0a`; image digest `sha256:392331bd92be12b4d74508c2d0cc706774ca12b256d3227acade55807ff7eff4`). It is a 390×844 populated launcher with 22 labeled app icons and the Ask Eliza bar. The same run's `report.json` records `domVerdict/verdict = good`, `readableChars = 415`, no console errors, no quality issues, and no overflow.

The OCR also reads the same icon-heavy surface as glyph noise at wider viewports, so this appears to be an OCR/preprocessing/confidence failure, not a render failure. Do not solve it by making genuinely blank pages pass.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Root-cause the OCR failure on the exact real launcher capture (preprocessing, scale, contrast/drop-shadow, segmentation mode, language/config, or verdict threshold) and document the measured cause.
- [ ] A populated `builtin-rolodex @ mobile-portrait` capture is never reported as "pixels are blank" when the actual pixels and independent DOM probe are healthy.
- [ ] A genuinely blank/transparent/solid-color capture still fails as `broken`; DOM-good alone must not fabricate an OCR success.
- [ ] OCR engine failure/low confidence is represented distinctly from a proven blank render (`needs-eyeball` or an explicit diagnostics error), with the raw OCR text/confidence retained.
- [ ] Regression coverage exercises the real capture/preprocessing/verdict path plus adversarial blank and unreadable cases; no stubbed OCR result stands in for the engine under test.
- [ ] Run `bun run --cwd packages/app audit:app` repeatedly on identical code and prove deterministic verdicts across every viewport; manually inspect the screenshots and reports.
- [ ] Attach the wrong capture/report first, then corrected desktop/mobile screenshots, OCR diagnostics, frontend logs, and a walkthrough artifact inline in the PR.

7:T782,## Symptom

In back-to-back `bun run --cwd packages/app audit:app` runs on the SAME commit (branch `fix/chat-widget-deslop`, 5-iteration loop for #15886), the Her-minimal ratchet (#9950) flipped on one view:

- Iterations 1 and 4: **pass** (0 ratchet violations, `plugin-inbox-gui` clean).
- Iterations 2, 3, 5: **fail** with exactly one violation — `plugin-inbox-gui @ mobile-landscape: border/divider density regressed 97.22 > baselined 72.91 + 5% tolerance (76.56)`.

The magnitude (≈33% over baseline vs clean-pass on identical code) suggests the view renders **different content** across runs — e.g. a divider-heavy populated list vs a sparser/empty state — not measurement jitter. It fires only at `mobile-landscape`.

Cross-checks:
- Two same-day CI `aesthetic-audit` runs on sibling branches from the same develop base (PRs #15889, #15894) show `plugin-inbox-gui` with **zero** ratchet violations at all four viewports.
- The branch under test changes only chat-widget/bubble styling in `packages/ui` — `plugins/plugin-inbox/src` imports none of the changed components (verified by grep), so the flip is not diff-induced.

## Why it matters

A coin-flip ratchet failure on an unrelated view fails the whole audit run (the gate is unconditional), which poisons the 5-loop grind required for UI PRs and trains people to ignore real ratchet regressions.

## Suggested fix directions

- Make the inbox GUI's audit fixture data deterministic at capture time (whatever feeds it rows appears to vary — check clock-relative filtering or unseeded data in the mocked /api fixtures for the inbox view at landscape widths).
- Or have the ratchet retry/flag single-view flips (same view passing and failing across consecutive same-commit runs) as `needs-eyeball` instead of failing the run.

## Scope

Package-dist assembly permits non-canonical local state to affect published output in two ways:

1. `prepare-package-dist.mjs` recursively discovers manifests. A tracked nested manifest such as `plugins/plugin-sql/src/package.json` can repeat a canonical workspace name with a different version and impersonate the workspace root during dependency rewriting.
2. `copy-package-assets.mjs` can copy generated Python cache, coverage, virtual-environment, and package-build residue into publish directories.

Resolve dependency versions through the repository's shared canonical workspace-discovery seam, reject duplicate canonical workspace identities, and exclude generic Python-generated residue while retaining source assets. This scope does not add generated packaging-runtime output rules or change application/runtime behavior.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] A nested manifest repeating a canonical workspace name cannot override the canonical workspace version.
- [ ] Duplicate names among canonical workspace roots fail with the conflicting identity and paths.
- [ ] Root workspace inclusions and negations remain the only source of workspace membership.
- [ ] Malformed selected workspace manifests fail rather than disappearing from dependency rewriting.
- [ ] `__pycache__`, `.pyc`/`.pyo`, Python test/type/lint caches, virtual environments, coverage residue, and `*.egg-info` directories are not copied.
- [ ] Ordinary Python source remains present.
- [ ] Focused tests cover the nested collision, duplicate and malformed canonical manifests, workspace negation, cache exclusions, and retained source.
- [ ] Representative agent, app-core, and UI builds and package dry-runs succeed.
- [ ] No package manifests, lockfiles, or workflows change.

## Evidence Plan

- [ ] Screenshots or recording: N/A - no rendered UI source or user flow changes.
- [ ] Logs: focused tests, shared workspace-seam tests, representative builds, and package dry-runs.
- [ ] Real-LLM trajectories: N/A - no model, prompt, provider, action, or agent-loop behavior changes.
- [ ] Domain artifacts: generated publish manifests, canonical-versus-emitted dependency versions, package summaries, and scans showing excluded residue.
- [ ] CI or local commands: Node 24 focused Vitest, Bun workspace-seam tests, Biome, error-policy ratchet, `git diff --check`, representative builds/packs, and full `bun run verify` after final sync.

Related: #13620, #13354, #14875, #14876.c:T103f,## Problem

Eliza currently has no canonical live lane that proves a **managed dedicated** Cloud agent can be provisioned on staging, answer a real proof-token chat turn through both bridge and SSE paths, and be cleaned up without silently exercising the shared tier.

This is a correctness/coverage bug, not a latency optimization. Several existing lanes appear to cover this path but do not:

- `hetzner-e2e.yml` is the only current workflow that combines a real proof-token `message.send` turn with raw-server teardown. It is blocked by the rejected isolated `HCLOUD_TOKEN_CI` (HTTP 401; #15616). The broader `HCLOUD_APPS_TOKEN` must not replace that scoped credential.
- `cloud-provisioning-e2e.mjs` and `live-cloud-provision-smoke.ts` create agents without `alwaysOn`/an equivalent dedicated-tier request. Current tier selection therefore defaults them to `shared`, even though their comments call the result Hetzner-backed.
- `cloud-provisioning-e2e.mjs` proves only `/api/status|health|me`; it does not send a real chat turn or delete the fresh agent.
- `live-cloud-provision-smoke.ts` has proof-token bridge/SSE checks and cleanup, but no workflow invokes it. With the current shared default, its pairing-token assertion is incompatible with the created tier. SIWE cleanup also leaves its disposable organization behind.
- `app-live-e2e.yml` wires `ELIZAOS_CLOUD_API_KEY`, while the repository has `ELIZACLOUD_API_KEY`. Recent cloud-login/provision/chat jobs report green with the sole Playwright test skipped because the key is empty (for example job `86628737358`). This is green-by-skip, not live evidence.
- `launch-hardening-8756.yml` and the mobile Cloud provisioning job are status-only and do not prove dedicated chat or full cleanup. Recent mobile success reused a shared agent and completed in about 1.3 seconds.

The staging Cloud bearer itself is healthy: the latest Hetzner E2E reached and passed the Cloud auth preflight before the independent Hetzner-token 401. Staging `/api/health` also serves the expected current commit. The missing piece is truthful harness wiring.

## Why this matters

Without a fail-closed dedicated lane, shared-runtime success and skipped tests can be mistaken for proof of the Worker → dedicated router → Headscale → agent path. That leaves the final acceptance in #15347 and the dedicated leg of #16079 unverified.

## Acceptance criteria

- Add one manual/scheduled staging workflow that uses the existing managed staging provisioner and the existing repository Cloud credential; do not require or substitute the broader apps-project Hetzner token.
- Create a unique canary explicitly as dedicated (`alwaysOn: true` or the current canonical equivalent) and require the returned tier/state to be dedicated, running, database-ready, heartbeat-fresh, and mesh-addressed.
- Fail closed if the credential is missing, a test skips, the response is a canned/fabricated fallback, or the actual tier/path differs from the declared dedicated path.
- Send nonce-bearing real turns through both central bridge and dedicated SSE. Require clean completion and proof match.
- Capture privacy-safe timings/trace/path fields only; never emit credentials, prompts, model output, raw provider bodies, agent IDs, or hostnames.
- In `finally`, delete only the unique agent created by the run and clean up the disposable user/organization. Cleanup failure must fail the lane or leave an explicit reaper artifact.
- Add a no-unbounded-autoscale/cost guard or an explicitly bounded canary-capacity contract.
- Fix the App Live secret-name drift and make a zero-executed-test/skip outcome red for the declared Cloud chat job.
- Correct stale “Hetzner-backed” comments/tests so shared and dedicated coverage cannot be confused.
- Link a successful exact-deploy-SHA run back to #15347 and #16079.

d:Ta40,## The layer under #15205
With the CF wildcard + TLS fixed (#15205), the app's per-agent calls now correctly reach `https://<id>.staging.elizacloud.ai` on the STAGING worker (verified: valid `*.staging.elizacloud.ai` cert, request lands on the staging cloud-api worker, no longer the prod one). But the chat still dead-ends: every `/api/*` call returns **HTTP 404 from Cloudflare with no CORS header** → browser reports `No 'Access-Control-Allow-Origin'`.

## Root cause (systemic, not per-agent)
The worker's `handleDedicatedAgentProxy` → `proxyToOrigin` forwards to `AGENT_ROUTER_ORIGIN_HOST = eliza-staging-1.elizacloud.ai` (the staging CP). The CP runs **nginx** (:80/:443) which routes `<id>.staging.elizacloud.ai` to the agent over the **headscale tailnet** via the agent's `headscale_ip`. But:
- **All 48 running staging agents have `headscale_ip` NULL/empty** (`SELECT (headscale_ip IS NULL OR headscale_ip='') , count(*) FROM agent_sandboxes WHERE status='running'` → `t | 48`).
- **`headscale nodes list` on the CP shows NO online agent-tagged node** — only `cp-staging-router` (tag:eliza-proxy, online) and two `robot-tstest`/`robot-tstest2` (tag:agent) **offline since 2026-06-16**.

So staging agent containers are provisioned + running + serving on their node bridge (`bridge_url` is set, e.g. `http://91.98.38.74:19006`), but they **never join the headscale mesh**, so the CP router has no tailnet address to reach them → 404. The agent's own CORS (`resolveCorsOrigin`, which allows any origin when `ELIZA_CLOUD_PROVISIONED=1`) is never reached because the request dies at the CP hop.

## Why it surfaced only now
Nobody ever got a staging agent reachable end-to-end before tonight (DB split #15160 → image boot #15196 → wildcard/TLS #15205 all had to land first). This is the next distinct gap in the same 'staging never fully mirrored prod' class, adjacent to the known prod-parity headscale gap.

## What to investigate

f:T592,Found while battery-testing every slash command through the GUI chat route (`POST /api/conversations/:id/messages/stream`).

**Repro:** in a conversation with enough history, send `/compact`. The deterministic command layer fires correctly (`thought: "Shortcut: cmd:compact"`), the COMPACT_CONVERSATION action succeeds, and its final text is the designed clean summary ("Compacted N older message(s); preserved the latest M."). But the **visible streamed reply** is the compactor's *internal* model call output — the raw rendered-ledger JSON:

```
```json
{ "state": { "facts": [ "…extracted facts from the conversation…" ] …
```

**Cause (as far as I traced):** the SSE route streams model tokens for the turn, and an action's *internal* `runtime.useModel` calls (here `buildActionCompactorModelCall`) stream into the same visible channel, so intermediate model output masquerades as the reply and the action's actual final text is buried. Any action that makes internal model calls on the GUI stream path has the same exposure. Discord/connector paths are unaffected (they deliver the discrete `reply` only).

This is the same family as the narration-envelope leaks (#8162): internal artifacts crossing into user-visible output. Expected behavior: mid-action internal model calls should not stream to the client; only the turn's actual reply should.

13:Tdb8,## Summary
`app-staging.elizacloud.ai` is stuck on **"initializing agent"** for every new agent. Root cause: **every agent-create call to the staging cloud-api returns HTTP 500 `{"code":"internal_error"}`**. The UI provisions the agent, then polls the per-agent proxy passthrough (`GET /api/conversations`) which never serves (the agent was never created), so `runCloudManagedWarmup()` spins on `initializing-agent` until the warmup deadline.

The 500 is an **unhandled DB/infrastructure error** inside the create handler, forced to the generic `internal_error` envelope by `isInfrastructureError()` in `cloud-worker-errors.ts` (so the real SQLSTATE/SQL message is swallowed to avoid leaking raw SQL).

## Reproduction (staging, authenticated session)
```
POST /api/v1/eliza/agents {"agentName":"x","autoProvision":false}  → 500 internal_error (t≈2–8s)
POST /api/v1/eliza/agents {"agentName":"x","autoProvision":true,"dockerImage":"ghcr.io/elizaos/eliza-agent:latest"} → 500 internal_error (t≈7.6s)
```
Both shared and dedicated create paths 500 → it's the create **write**, not provisioning/worker-health.

## Reads are healthy — this is write/transaction-specific
```
GET /api/v1/eliza/agents    → 200 {"data":[]}   (t spiky 0.27–6.4s, but succeeds)
GET /api/v1/credits/balance → 200 {"balance":…} (t≈1.3s)
GET /api/health             → 200 environment:staging
```
- Bogus `dockerImage` → **403 agent_image_not_allowed** (handler reaches deep into `createAgent`/`assertAgentImageAllowed`; the write AFTER the allowlist gate is what fails).
- `getReadConnection()` and `getWriteConnection()` both resolve the **same** `getPrimaryDatabaseUrl()`/Hyperdrive binding, so this is NOT a code-level read/write DB split. The difference: create runs a **multi-statement interactive `dbWrite.transaction`** with a Postgres advisory lock + `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` + INSERT (`eliza-sandbox.ts` `createAgent`), while a passing read is a single pooled query.

## Ruled out
- **Schema drift** — deploy migrations job: `[db:migrate] pending migrations: 0; migrations complete`; deployed branch has migrations through `0176`.
- **General DB outage** — reads succeed.
- **Deployed branch code** — staging is currently deployed from `fix/cloud-cerebras-reasoning-effort` (PR #16087) via `workflow_dispatch`; that PR touches zero create/sandbox/db-client/managed-env files.

## Most likely cause
Interactive transaction / advisory-lock write over the **staging Hyperdrive binding `ef65eb94c74144a3a3848ad1945571fb`** failing (SQLSTATE swallowed to generic 500), while simple pooled reads pass. Same failure class as the previously-closed #15160 / #15203 staging-provisioning DB incidents.

## Needs (holder-gated — I don't have elizaOS CF account access)
1. `wrangler tail eliza-cloud-api-staging --env staging --format json` while issuing a create, to capture the swallowed Postgres SQLSTATE behind `[CloudApi] Unhandled error`.
2. Verify staging Hyperdrive binding `ef65eb94c74144a3a3848ad1945571fb` points at the intended **staging** Postgres and supports interactive transactions (re-point if stale/misconfigured — the #15160 surface).
3. Consider reverting staging to `develop` (currently on the cerebras feature branch) so QA tests real staging code.

Once the SQLSTATE is known: a code-level Hyperdrive-transaction incompatibility is a bounded fix; a binding/DB-target misconfig is an ops flip.

_Diagnosed by [sol-cloud]. No prod changes, no staging redeploy, no DB writes persisted._
14:Ta2e,## Problem

The core task scheduler can make a broken queue look healthy:

- a transient `getTasks` rejection clears the dirty bit before the query and can leave local or shared schedulers permanently silent;
- missing task IDs/workers, rejected or non-boolean `shouldRun` results, and malformed schedule values are skipped or coerced instead of surfacing;
- manual worker failures can be recorded without being returned to the caller;
- repeat backoff can compound from an already-inflated interval, and malformed/adaptive zero intervals can create busy loops;
- the shared scheduler trusts each returned row's `agentId`, so an adapter boundary violation can dispatch a task outside the query's tenant scope;
- detached timer failures are logged but do not consistently enter `runtime.reportError` / `RECENT_ERRORS`.

This conflicts with the fail-fast/error-observability policy in #12182 and makes LifeOps heartbeat failures especially hard to diagnose.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Local and shared queue-query failures re-arm every still-registered affected agent and surface a typed `ElizaError` through the runtime error channel.
- [ ] A healthy task still runs when another row fails validation, then the tick reports the complete typed failure aggregate.
- [ ] Missing IDs/workers, invalid preflight results, invalid due/repeat timestamps, non-positive intervals, invalid failure metadata, and invalid adaptive intervals never become healthy skips or fabricated defaults.
- [ ] Manual task execution rejects after its durable success/failure transition; state-write failures retain both the execution and persistence causes.
- [ ] Repeat backoff uses the original base cadence, resets on success, and preserves explicit never-auto-pause semantics.
- [ ] Shared scheduling never dispatches a row whose owner is missing or outside the exact queried agent set; the violation remains observable and re-armed.
- [ ] Timer, shared-daemon, and serverless/manual paths have regression coverage, including transient DB failure, malformed persisted rows, multi-agent isolation, and worker/state failure.
- [ ] `@elizaos/core` typecheck and Node/browser/edge/testing/declaration builds pass; diff-scoped error-policy and formatting gates pass.
- [ ] PR evidence includes inspected task-state artifacts and structured error logs from a real persisted scheduler run. UI/video/model trajectory rows are explicitly marked N/A when inapplicable.

## Non-goals

This does not add another scheduler or change the `ScheduledTask` architecture. It hardens the existing `TaskService` and shared scheduler boundaries.
15:Ta5a,Split out of #15855 (commenter-reported failure mode 3). The other two failure modes in that issue are handled: initial-login retry landed in #15940/#16022, and inbound drop-path instrumentation + gateway drop recovery is in its own PR (see #15855 thread).

## Problem

A process restart landing mid-turn silently eats the in-flight request and leaves the status reaction orphaned:

- At `systemctl restart` (or any SIGTERM) while a turn is mid-planner-call, journald shows `Stopping → Stopped → Started` in ~1s; the agent log shows the planner surface being built, then `error: script "dev" was terminated by signal SIGTERM`.
- The user's request vanishes — no reply, ever.
- The 🤔 status reaction placed at turn start stays on the message forever, so the bot looks stuck.
- Restarts additionally open a ~1–2 min double-responder window when the old gateway session outlives "Stopped" (two different replies to one question observed live; the older process's logs are lost with its buffers).

## Proposed direction (from the #15855 thread)

Graceful drain on SIGTERM:

1. Stop accepting new inbound messages (connectors pause ingestion; the new inbound-drop reporter gives this a designed-ignore reason so paused ingestion is observable, not silent).
2. Let in-flight turns finish within a bounded drain window (e.g. 15–30s).
3. For turns that will not finish: clear or annotate their status reactions ("restarting, please re-send") so no reaction is orphaned.
4. Exit only after the drain window closes.
5. The supervisor chain (`bun run dev` → dev-ui → agent) must forward SIGTERM and honor the drain window instead of hard-killing children.
6. On startup, reconcile leftover status reactions from a previous unclean shutdown (bounded scan of recent bot reactions) so a hard kill (SIGKILL/OOM) does not leave permanent 🤔 markers either.

## Acceptance criteria

- Deterministic test: SIGTERM during an in-flight Discord turn → turn completes and the reply is delivered when it fits the drain window.
- Deterministic test: turn exceeding the drain window → status reaction cleared/annotated and a "restarting, please re-send" marker delivered before exit.
- No new inbound accepted after SIGTERM is received (observable via the structured inbound-drop reporter, not a silent gate).
- Supervisor chain forwards the signal end-to-end (dev-ui and packaged runtimes), proven with real process logs.
- Live evidence: a real restart during a live turn showing drain behavior in backend logs.

## Non-goals

- Initial-login retry (done, #15940/#16022).
- Post-ready silent drop instrumentation/recovery (separate PR under #15855).

17:T2133,# Relates to

Relates to #13580 and #13581. This closes the agent-resolvable hosted-emulator and evidence-delivery residuals. The arm64 LOCAL round trip remains open until a runner with labels `self-hosted`, `linux`, `ARM64`, `android-device` is provisioned.

Definition of Done: full standard in `CONTRIBUTING.md`.

- [x] Targets `develop`; exact head `e44bd654f26` is based on current `origin/develop` (`0f8045c6744d6dd696a57ba0a053f9f2ee55355c`).

- [x] The exact-head mandatory app audit passed 246/246 views with no broken or needs-work verdicts.
- [ ] The exact-head API-34 PR/device and full-hosted emulator runs are in progress; every resulting artifact will be opened before the draft is promoted.

# What this fixes

The Android lane had several independent false-proof paths:

- `reactivecircus/android-emulator-runner` executes each nonblank script line in a separate `sh -c`, so multiline Bash state, traps, PIDs, continuations, and `pipefail` were lost;
- a cold native `appUrlOpen` could arrive while the startup splash listener was mounted but still restoring reset/session state; reading `gatewayUrl` falsely acknowledged the command immediately before restore cleared the newly selected remote;
- hosted emulators expose a hardware keyboard, suppressing the real software IME even after the probe requested it, and the verifier sampled UIAutomator only once;
- Android toybox did not finalize `screenrecord` through the previous `pkill -INT` path, leaving an `ftyp/free/mdat` file with no `moov` box;
- failed verifier commands and non-finalized recordings could still look like uploaded proof.

The branch moves emulator work into committed Bash processes, queues and drains early native deep links, exposes renderer readiness, acknowledges/replays CONNECT delivery within a bounded window, and ensures only the correct startup owner can claim a deep link. It also forces the emulator software-IME policy, polls the actual IME hierarchy, terminates `screenrecord` by PID with SIGINT, waits for encoder/transport exit, and rejects incomplete ISO-BMFF output.

# Final remote-loopback correction

The final hosted run exposed one more device-only ambiguity: the remote-mac harness deliberately forwards the host through `http://127.0.0.1:31337`, while the Android client previously treated every loopback-shaped URL as the bundled native agent. That hijacked the forwarded request and returned `local_agent_unavailable` even though the remote coordinator was healthy.

The exact head now resolves Android routing from persisted runtime mode, durable Cloud-session precedence, and build defaults before deciding whether loopback HTTP belongs to the native bridge. IPC remains unambiguously native. The mounted-App regression drives a real `CONNECT_EVENT` through deferred first-run adoption and asserts the observable `adopt → complete → retry` outcome sequence, remote mode, shell state, and success notice.

Final local gates: 10 changed UI test files pass 143/143; the enforced changed-file coverage gate remains above 50% for every executable target (81.05% mean for the final routing slice); test-realness reports zero regressions; and `bun run verify` passes 573/573 Turbo tasks plus all audits and 28/28 dist-path consumers.

Start with:

1. `.github/workflows/android-device-e2e.yml` and `scripts/mobile/android-emulator-webview-ci.sh`;
2. `packages/app/src/mobile-lifecycle.ts`, `packages/ui/src/events/index.ts`, and `packages/ui/src/state/use-startup-shell-controller.ts`;
3. `packages/app/scripts/lib/android-capture.mjs` and `packages/app/scripts/android-assistant-verify.mjs`;
4. the exact-head Actions runs and downloaded artifacts linked below once complete.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Adds bounded, privacy-safe, trace-correlated timings for credential extraction, cache availability/read/write, API-key lookup, user/org lookup, moderation, and total auth resolution.
- Makes inference-auth cache availability and outcomes explicit instead of conflating unavailable/malformed data with a healthy miss.
- Uses a focused inference API-key resolver that preserves the existing errors, validation order, and usage accounting while allowing controlled probes and cache-failure recovery to measure authoritative repositories.
- Defers only the post-authorization positive KV population write to the Worker lifetime, with a second correlated completion record for the actual backend outcome and duration.
- Invalidates positive auth entries across API-key lifecycle and moderation changes.
- Adds an exact-version staging probe and GitHub workflow that deploys a uniquely named workers.dev-only diagnostic Worker, creates an isolated auth fixture, captures 30 retained hits + 10 controlled misses + fail-closed guards, sanitizes Tail logs, enforces thresholds, and always removes the Worker and fixture.
- Proves Worker Tail readiness with a real authenticated trace before retaining samples. The readiness boundary retries only the observed transient workers.dev propagation 404, fails immediately on other HTTP statuses, and emits only sanitized Wrangler stderr on exhaustion.
- Adds a narrow Gitleaks exception for the deterministic public `unit-probe-token:<32 lowercase hex>` test fixture in its single test file; the rest of that file and the full branch range remain scanned.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix and performance improvement for the cloud inference-auth hot path, plus diagnostic/test infrastructure.

# Documentation changes needed?

No public documentation change is needed. The new workflow and probe are self-describing operational diagnostics, and the runtime contract remains unchanged.

## Detailed testing steps

The final exact-version run produced:

| Auth interval | n | p50 | p90 | p95 | max |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Exact-version cache hit | 30 | 2 ms | 3 ms | 3 ms | 6 ms |
| Controlled cache miss | 10 | 271 ms | 281 ms | 406 ms | 406 ms |

The controlled-miss p90 is **91.2% lower** than the issue's 3,204 ms evidence, with no multi-second auth tail. Controlled-miss stage p90s: key 57 ms, user/org 102 ms, moderation 53 ms, KV read 73 ms. Deferred KV writes completed at p90 254 ms; 10/10 were observed as `written`. The single 890 ms deferred-write maximum remained outside the response path.

Worker Tail readiness required six authenticated probes before capture. Only after that trace appeared did the workflow retain the 43 measured requests, eliminating the process-live/websocket-not-ready race that an earlier diagnostic run exposed.

Fail-closed guards in the same run:

- Invalid key: HTTP 401, authoritative `rejected`, write `not_run`.
- Suspended key: HTTP 403, authoritative `suspended`, write `not_run`.
- Forged controlled-probe request: HTTP 400, cached authorization with probe `off`; the request could not force an authoritative miss.

The [pre-optimization exact-version run](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/29314229862) measured controlled-miss p90 679 ms and KV-write p90 277 ms, isolating the positive KV write as the dominant controllable boundary.

19:T2914,## Summary

- run the scheduled iOS Local lane through the canonical composition: fresh simulator app install, OS-delivered auth callback, then the strict full-Bun local-model probe
- write/read Capacitor preferences inside the simulator domain, repair the Xcode 16.4 simulator Swift-library lookup for `simctl` children, and pre-approve the app's URL scheme without replacing unrelated LaunchServices entries
- share one strict app-core full-Bun probe with the renderer, preserve the exact model input/output receipt, and render an explicit terminal pass/fail state only after the terminal receipt is persisted
- split platform entrypoint seams into source modules with honest source-path tests; changed-source coverage passes without a classifier or policy exemption
- hard-gate the redacted scheme/auth/model receipts, exact head and model hashes, unified logs, JPG, and H.264 MP4

Addresses #13577. Scoped contribution to #13562.

## Root cause and repair

The hosted lane exposed three independent simulator boundaries in sequence:

1. A host-path `defaults write` could exit successfully without producing simulator-readable Capacitor Preferences. The lane now executes `xcrun simctl spawn <udid> defaults ...`, supports Capacitor's prefixed key plus its raw alias, and requires a native readback before launch.
2. Xcode 16.4 with the iOS 18.5 simulator could launch-abort while resolving `/usr/lib/swift/libswiftWebKit.dylib`. The workflow supplies Apple's simulator-runtime Cryptex fallback only to `simctl` child processes; it does not change the production deployment target.
3. A fresh simulator presented an untappable `Open in “Eliza”?` sheet for `simctl openurl`, leaving auth at `phase=requested`. The orchestrator now preserves the existing LaunchServices dictionary, writes and reads back `com.apple.CoreSimulator.CoreSimulatorBridge-->elizaos = ai.elizaos.app`, emits a redacted receipt, and reboots once when that mapping changes.

Mutation-sensitive contracts prove unapproved=`confirmation-blocked`, approved=`deliver-to-app`, wrong-bundle repair, idempotence, simulator-native preference readback, and the complete strict non-streaming + streaming model contract.

# Risks

**Medium.** This changes GitHub credential ownership, startup hydration, device-flow routes, and client refresh behavior. The main risks are cross-agent credential disclosure, accepting a cancelled/superseded grant, losing an existing credential during reconnect, and stale Octokit/git clients. The tests exercise two-agent isolation, encrypted persistence, restart hydration, cancellation/supersession races, typed OAuth failures, reconnect preservation, and client refresh generation guards.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Stores GitHub OAuth/PAT credentials in the existing encrypted vault under agent-scoped keys and hydrates only the matching runtime without mutating `process.env`.
- Replaces placeholder device authorization behavior with typed start/poll/cancel/reconnect routes. Device codes stay server-side behind opaque local flow IDs; expiry, denial, cancellation, `slow_down`, upstream timeout, and newest-flow-wins behavior are explicit.
- Revalidates a flow after an in-flight GitHub token poll so cancellation or supersession cannot revive and persist that flow.
- Validates granted tokens through GitHub `/user`, commits to the vault before refreshing runtime clients, and preserves the current credential when reconnect fails.
- Removes app-core's host-global credential file/environment bootstrap and prevents orchestrator git children from inheriting ambient GitHub token variables. Authenticated git commands receive the selected agent credential through a per-command `extraheader`.
- Makes the guided credential the default agent GitHub identity while retaining explicit role/account precedence and PAT entry.
- Renders distinct loading, empty, unavailable/error, waiting, cancelled, denied, expired, success, and reconnect states.
- Updates the connector and package documentation, including the single-agent-only limitation of owner-provided environment configuration.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking behavior repair for agent isolation and guided GitHub OAuth).

# Documentation changes needed?

Documentation was updated in `packages/docs/connectors/github.md` and the affected plugin READMEs/package guides.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

1. Review the desktop/mobile GitHub card captures below, especially waiting, cancelled, expired, denied, unavailable/retry, success, and connected/reconnect.
2. With the elizaOS OAuth App client ID configured as `GITHUB_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID`, start Device Flow for agent A, approve it, and confirm agent A becomes connected.
3. In the same process, confirm agent B remains disconnected and cannot poll, read, overwrite, or clear agent A's flow/credential.
4. Perform a real GitHub operation as agent A, restart the runtime, and confirm the same identity is hydrated from the encrypted vault.
5. Reconnect agent A and deny/cancel the grant; confirm the existing identity remains active.

# Risks

**Medium.** The production change maps JSON object requests to the AI SDK v6 `Output.json()` contract so `response_format` actually reaches OpenAI-compatible providers; non-Cerebras schema output remains schema-backed while Cerebras uses its supported schema-free JSON mode. The evidence proxy records raw request/response bytes, so its credential and cookie redaction is security-sensitive. Unit tests exercise split headers/bodies, binary integrity, signal forwarding, and bounded fault injection, and the uploaded exact-head artifact was scanned manually for credentials.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

It closes the proof gaps left by #16299 with a reproducible Cerebras evidence lane:

- preserves redacted raw request, response, and SSE chunk bytes with length and SHA-256 receipts;
- records real LLM trajectories for reasoning, exact GLM streaming, forced tool use, and structured JSON output;
- proves real 401, 404, 400 context-limit, caller-abort, and terminal mid-stream disconnect behavior;
- registers the credentialed suite in the real/live manifest and uploads evidence even when a later carrier step fails;
- fixes AI SDK v6 structured-output plumbing so the intended `response_format: {"type":"json_object"}` is present on the actual Cerebras wire;
- applies the disconnect fault to every bounded SDK retry so the error-path test cannot recover into false green.

The first exact run failed three assertions and is retained as a regression receipt: https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/29303733035. The repaired exact-head run passes the scoped provider lane: https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/29305529434.

Bug fix plus live evidence/test infrastructure.

## Manual artifact review

I downloaded both final-run ZIPs and verified their SHA-256 values match GitHub's digests. I opened `report.json`, the HTML viewer/data, stdout/stderr, both JSONL files, the raw-wire JSON, and the sweep log. All 42 byte receipts reproduced their recorded byte lengths, SHA-256 values, and UTF-8 views. Authorization and set-cookie values are `[REDACTED]`; no Cerebras-key or bearer-credential pattern occurs in the extracted bundles. All nine exercised receipts passed, all three SDK retry attempts ended in injected disconnect after one real upstream chunk, and the 429 row remains explicitly `not-exercised`.1d:T1a29,## Root cause

When mobile voice explicitly forces Eliza Cloud TTS (`provider === "eliza-cloud"`), the renderer still POSTed to the on-device agent's `/api/tts/cloud` proxy. On a dedicated/on-device (hybrid) runtime that proxy runs on the constrained device itself, so every reply pays an extra audio download through the phone-side Bun event loop **plus a base64 IPC re-marshal** — measured at ~5–6s/reply even though the cloud worker completes in 1–2s. The worker's provider-agnostic `POST /api/v1/voice/tts` route could serve the same `{ text }` → audio bytes directly.

Before this change, `useVoiceChat.speakElizaCloud` only bypassed the proxy for **shared-runtime** agents (`#15395`); a dedicated/on-device agent always went `resolveApiUrl("/api/tts/cloud")` → local proxy → cloud.

## The fix

`speakElizaCloud` now resolves its target through a **pure** `resolveForcedCloudTtsRoute(...)` helper (`packages/ui/src/voice/shared-runtime-voice.ts`), which picks one of three targets:

1. **shared-runtime** → the worker's `/api/v1/voice/tts` off the active base (unchanged, `#15395`).
2. **direct-cloud** (`#16116`, new) → when a **cloud session bearer** (`getCloudAuthToken()` — the canonical Steward JWT) **and** a **configured cloud origin** (`configuredCloudVoiceOrigin()`, derived from boot config `cloudApiBase`) are both present, POST straight to `<cloudOrigin>/api/v1/voice/tts` with `Authorization: Bearer <cloud session token>`, skipping the on-device proxy entirely.
3. **on-device-proxy** → when cloud auth/config is unavailable, the existing `/api/tts/cloud` path is preserved unchanged.

The cloud origin is read from boot config (**not** a hardcoded production URL), so staging/custom cloud environments route correctly. Only the URL + bearer change — the `{ text }` body and audio-bytes response are identical.

### Acceptance criteria
- [x] Forced-cloud TTS uses the cloud session bearer token and configured cloud API origin.
- [x] Production is not hardcoded over staging/custom boot config (origin derived from `cloudApiBase`).
- [x] Existing non-forced/proxy behavior remains unchanged (fallback to `/api/tts/cloud` when no cloud auth/config; other providers untouched).
- [x] Focused headless regression proves routing and fallback.

## Files
- `packages/ui/src/voice/shared-runtime-voice.ts` — `configuredCloudVoiceOrigin()`, `resolveForcedCloudTtsRoute()`, `ForcedCloudTtsRoute`.
- `packages/ui/src/hooks/useVoiceChat.ts` — `speakElizaCloud` routes via the resolver; direct path carries the cloud bearer.
- `packages/ui/src/voice/shared-runtime-voice.test.ts` — unit coverage (origin helper + resolver, all branches).
- `packages/ui/src/hooks/useVoiceChat.forced-cloud-tts.test.tsx` — hook-level regression driving the real hook/processQueue.

## How to verify
On a device running a local/on-device Eliza agent, connect Eliza Cloud (so a Steward session token exists) and set voice provider to `eliza-cloud`. Speak. The forced-cloud TTS request now hits `<cloudApiBase>/api/v1/voice/tts` directly with the cloud bearer — it no longer appears against the on-device `/api/tts/cloud` proxy (`ELIZA_TTS_DEBUG` logs `useVoiceChat:eliza-cloud-direct-worker`). Log out of Cloud (no session token) and the request falls back to `/api/tts/cloud`, unchanged.

- Base: `4f766c5b71f16f0a143e495b7fb19f09cd1e67f8`
- `git fetch origin` confirms the remote branch and local head are identical;
  `origin/develop` is unchanged from the branch merge-base.
- `bun run verify` passes at the exact head: 573/573 Turbo tasks and 28/28
  dist-path consumer configs.

# Risks

Medium. This deliberately makes the live gate stricter. A reported runtime or
service error, failed action, structured failure reply, errored/active native
trajectory, or missing required native artifact now fails the benchmark after
the review bundle is persisted.

Designed confirmation and clarification results remain valid terminal states.
A tool result with `requiresConfirmation` is accepted only when it is an
error-free confirmation or carries a recognized missing/ambiguous-input code;
a structured service error still fails even if it also asks for confirmation.
The hosted capability profile explicitly declines authenticated calendar,
meeting-corpus, and native screen-time cases instead of fabricating empty data
or mutating an external owner account.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Gives each selected case a typed terminal outcome: `completed`, explicit
  `capability_unavailable`, or typed `failed`.
- Captures runtime reports, failed action events, structured failure replies,
  native tool failures, trajectory aborts, and incomplete trajectories, then
  validates them fail-closed.
- Registers the production-owned knowledge graph in the real LifeOps PGLite
  test host and verifies that collaborator directly.
- Defines and tests explicit host/mobile action capability profiles.
- Uses the real, non-mutating `schedule_plan` path (`list_active`) for the hosted
  capability case instead of dispatching to an unavailable counterparty.
- Persists `report.json`, `report.md`, `cases.jsonl`, every native JSON/Markdown
  trajectory, and a SHA-256 manifest before the terminal assertion, including
  red runs.
- Uploads that bundle on every credentialed live workflow dispatch and runs the
  PR workflow against `develop`.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix and benchmark-evidence hardening.

# Documentation changes needed?

No user documentation change is needed. The workflow, typed rows, report, and
manifest are the review contract for this test-only infrastructure.

# Risks

Medium: this introduces the future stable merge-gate context. The aggregate is
base-trusted, read-only, hosted, bounded to 45 minutes, and never checks out PR
code. It binds every observed check to the GitHub Actions app, exact owning
workflow path, `pull_request` event, and current PR head SHA.

This PR does **not** mutate ruleset `18901247`. The existing nine no-bypass
required contexts remain authoritative throughout bootstrap and live canaries.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Adds one always-emitted `Develop PR Gate` check for PRs targeting `develop`.
It polls the nine existing lightweight required contexts and passes only when
all nine have a current, owner-bound terminal `success`. Missing, stale,
pending past the poll budget, cancelled, timed out, failed, neutral, action
required, and skipped results fail closed. Owner workflows may classify
non-applicable work inside an always-emitted successful job; disappearing
behind a path filter is forbidden by the static workflow contract.

The workflow uses `pull_request_target` only as a trusted orchestration
boundary: it checks out `pull_request.base.sha`, has read-only permissions, has
no secrets, and observes check metadata for `pull_request.head.sha`. The new
self-test is also wired into the already-required `Test Integrity (lane
coverage)` job so this bootstrap PR validates the proposed implementation even
though a newly-added base-trusted workflow cannot execute until it exists on
the base branch.

The workflow README now distinguishes the lightweight pre-merge surface from
the exhaustive post-merge suites and the push-only `ci-ok` roll-up.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix / CI enforcement improvement.

# Documentation changes needed?

Updated `.github/workflows/README.md` with the current pre-merge and post-merge
ownership model.

# Testing

   transitions and trust-boundary tamper cases.

   boundary.

   trigger, path-filter, permissions, and checkout contracts.

Deterministic terminal-state receipt at this exact head:

| Scenario | Expected aggregate | Observed |
| --- | --- | --- |
| all nine success | success | success / exit 0 |
| owner-bound context missing at deadline | failure | `missing` |
| context remains in progress at deadline | failure | `pending-timeout` |
| cancelled | failure | `terminal-cancelled` |
| timed out | failure | `terminal-timed_out` |
| failed | failure | `terminal-failure` |
| skipped/path-disappeared | failure | `skipped-forbidden` |
| wrong workflow path or wrong app | wait, then fail missing | observed |
| stale result after an event that reruns its owner | wait, then fail stale | observed |
| newer successful rerun supersedes an old failure | success | observed |

Real GitHub baseline canaries for the same nine source contexts are preserved
in #13306: [green #16321](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/pull/16321/checks)
reached 9/9 success, including the
[zero-file coverage job](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/29298277609/job/86976439814);
[red #16322](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/pull/16322) was blocked by the
[failed title job](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/29298342833/job/86976635515).

## Post-merge aggregate canaries and migration (required before ruleset mutation)

1. Dispatch `develop-pr-gate.yml` on `develop` once for each synthetic
   `success`, `missing`, `cancelled`, `timed-out`, `failed`, `skipped`, and
   `pending-timeout` scenario. Only `success` may conclude success.
2. Open aggregate-specific green and deliberately red PR canaries. Confirm the
   aggregate observes real owner checks and that the red source lane makes the
   aggregate red.
3. Add `Develop PR Gate` to ruleset `18901247` **alongside** the existing nine
   contexts. Confirm a PR is blocked while it is missing/pending and becomes
   mergeable only after both old and new gates pass.
4. Only after those receipts, replace the nine individual required contexts
   with the aggregate. At no point is the rule open or bypassable.

Rollback is the reverse, fail-closed sequence: restore the captured nine
individual contexts alongside the aggregate first, verify effective-rule
readback and a green PR, then remove the aggregate context. If the aggregate
is unhealthy, leave the old nine in force. Never delete/disable the ruleset;
its `bypass_actors: []`, PR, deletion, and non-fast-forward rules remain
unchanged.

## Known gaps / failures

- The `pull_request_target` aggregate cannot execute on the PR that first adds
  it because the trusted base does not contain it. The existing required lane
  executes its complete self-test/contract on this PR; live aggregate dispatch
  and PR canaries happen after merge and before any ruleset mutation.
- A historical replay attempted after #16321/#16322 source branches were
  deleted returned GitHub API 422 because their head SHAs were no longer
  reachable refs. Their permanent Actions job URLs and ruleset results remain
  linked above; this is not a product or test failure.20:T1a23,Fixes #16065. Reworks the closed #15998 per the reviewer's requests (error-classified retry + committed tests + a clean touched suite).

## Root cause

A committed `cloud-hybrid` mobile runtime re-onboarded on **every** cold launch, via two independent mechanisms:

1. **Persisted-record path** — `reconcileMobileRestoredActiveServer` (`packages/ui/src/state/startup-phase-restore.ts`) accepted the on-device active-server record only for mode `"local"`. For `cloud-hybrid` it returned `null`, which cleared the record + reset `firstRunComplete` on every boot — bouncing a returning hybrid user into onboarding.
2. **Probe path** — when no active server was persisted (e.g. a WebView-storage wipe, #11506), `detectExistingFirstRunConnection` (`packages/ui/src/state/first-run-bootstrap.ts`) gave up after a fixed **3.5s** single-shot, while the on-device agent takes **~30s** to boot. It read the still-booting agent as "no install" → onboarding.

## What the reviewer rejected on #15998 (and this reworks)

The #15998 retry swallowed **every** probe failure as "still booting" and, after up to 45s, converted it into first-run — so an auth `401`, a real `5xx`, or malformed JSON became onboarding instead of surfacing. J4 can't turn arbitrary failures into an "unavailable" result. The reviewer also required committed retry-loop tests, a symmetric Android below-floor diagnostic for cloud-hybrid, and a clean touched suite.

## The fix

- **reconcile** — accept the on-device record for **both** committed on-device modes (`local` + `cloud-hybrid`) through one shared predicate `isCommittedOnDeviceMobileRuntimeMode` (`packages/ui/src/first-run/mobile-runtime-mode.ts`); non-agent modes (`cloud`/`remote-mac`/`tunnel-to-mobile`) are still dropped as stale.
- **error-classified retry** — `detectExistingFirstRunConnection` gains `waitForBootingAgent`. It keeps retrying **only** while the agent is still booting, classified by the new exported `isBootingAgentProbeError`, which reuses the exact transient band `startup-phase-poll.ts` already trusts: `ApiError` kind `network`/`timeout`, or HTTP `502`/`503`/`504` (the iOS/Android native transports answer a not-yet-ready kernel with a structured `503`). Any **genuine** fault — auth `401/403`, a real `500`, `parse` on malformed JSON, or an unrecognized throw — is **rethrown**, never retried into first-run. The fresh-install single-shot is unchanged (instant onboarding).
- **restore boundary (J1)** — the probe call site catches a rethrown genuine fault and, because the committed install exists, routes to restore-as-detected-install so the **polling-backend phase surfaces the real error** through its designed timeout/error states, instead of masking it as onboarding.
- **Android** — `shouldAutoStart` now pre-warms the bundled agent at boot for `cloud-hybrid` (4 GB hybrid floor) as well as `local` (8 GB), reusing the existing `allowsAgentStartForRuntimeMode`; the fail-loud below-floor diagnostic now covers **both** modes symmetrically, so a below-floor cloud-hybrid is refused observably rather than leaving the renderer to wait out the 45s probe.

## Tests — real functions, injected transports (nothing under test is mocked)

- `packages/ui/src/state/first-run-bootstrap.test.ts` (new, 12 tests): classifier table (transient vs genuine vs unknown); repeated-unreachable → eventual success (no re-onboard); timeout termination → onboard; cancellation stops probing after the race settles; genuine `401`/`parse` faults **surfaced (thrown)**, not retried; fresh-install single-shot never waits and even an auth error resolves instantly.
- `packages/ui/src/state/persistence-cloud-active-server.test.ts` (+3): cloud-hybrid and local on-device records kept; non-agent modes (`cloud`/`remote-mac`) still dropped.
- `packages/app-core/.../ElizaAgentAutostartPolicyTest.java`: cloud-hybrid autostarts at the 4 GB floor, local at 8 GB, below-floor refused, fresh install never; `isOnDeviceAgentRuntimeMode` predicate.

21:T19e0,## Summary

Fixes #16242. A fresh, unauthenticated production browser context on the shared Eliza Cloud web app (`app.elizacloud.ai`) renders onboarding but probes **protected** agent endpoints **before** Cloud sign-in. Each returns `401` and Chromium logs it as a console error — the noisy first-run logs users report.

Protected probes that fired before auth (all 401):

| Endpoint | Fired from |
| --- | --- |
| `GET /api/first-run/status` | existing-install boot probe |
| `GET /api/config` | existing-install boot probe |
| `GET /api/notifications?limit=100` | notification store hydrate |
| `GET /api/runtime/mode` | `useRuntimeMode` |
| `GET /api/commands?surface=gui` | slash-command catalog |
| `GET /api/custom-actions` | slash-command catalog |
| `GET /api/location/approximate` | weather home widget |

## Root cause

The dashboard is kept **warm-but-hidden** during onboarding, so its shell-boot hooks/widgets mount and fetch on mount unconditionally. Separately, the boot **existing-install** probe (`detectExistingFirstRunConnection` → `GET /api/first-run/status` + `/api/config`) runs even when the same-origin API is the auth-gated Eliza Cloud control plane — where there is no unauthenticated local install to detect. `/api/auth/me` is the one probe that degrades gracefully (not in the list); it's the designated auth check.

## The fix

One origin-aware gate, reusing the exact predicate the app already uses for `isAgentlessCloudOrigin` (`isElizaCloudControlPlaneAgentlessBase`) plus the established `useIsAuthenticated()` (#11084 doctrine: "shell data loaders must not start until [authenticated]"):

```
probesEnabled = authenticated || !isElizaCloudControlPlaneAgentlessBase(window.location.origin)
```

- **New `useProtectedAgentProbesEnabled()`** gates the React shell hooks: `useWeather`, `useRuntimeMode`, `useSlashCommandController`.
- **`detectExistingFirstRunConnection`** gates itself via the new pure `shouldProbeExistingLocalInstall(origin)` — it returns `null` (no probe) on a bare Cloud control-plane origin.
- **The notification store** hydrate is gated at its own `.ts` module seam (`requestHydration`) using the same gate read via new non-hook `isAuthenticatedNow()` / `subscribeAuthStatus()`, with a **one-shot re-arm** so the inbox hydrates the moment a session lands after sign-in.

The gate is **inert everywhere except a bare, unauthenticated Cloud control-plane origin**. Local / self-hosted / desktop / remote agents (no cloud auth wall, so no 401) and authenticated Cloud sessions probe exactly as before, and the probes **resume the moment a session exists** after sign-in (effects re-run / the store re-arms). No global suppression — genuine failures still surface (`Do not suppress genuine failures globally`).

### Endpoints now gated

# Risks

**Medium.** The classifier and OCR primitive are shared by every app-audit screenshot. A weak whole-page read now incurs one bounded high-contrast/sparse-text retry, which adds OCR work only below the two-word/45% reliability floor. True solid/transparent blank frames still fail from independent pixel evidence. Low-confidence nonblank frames remain visible as `needs-eyeball`; they are not promoted to healthy.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

The old rule inferred “pixels are blank” from a short OCR transcript. On the exact 390×844 issue capture (`sha256:392331bd…eff4`), whole-page packaged Tesseract saw one word, `oY)`, at 40% confidence even though 22 labeled launcher icons and the Ask Eliza bar were visibly rendered.

This change separates the signals:

- pixel analysis alone can prove blank pixels (no samples or one quantized color);
- weak OCR gets one deterministic retry: up to 3×/2400 px, normalized grayscale, sharpen, threshold 225, sparse-text segmentation;
- selected mode, raw attempt transcripts, word/character counts, and mean confidence remain in `ocr-triage.json`;
- weak OCR on nonblank pixels becomes `needs-eyeball`; OCR/image diagnostics failures remain explicit errors;
- imported OCR is enriched from the report-authorized screenshot path, never record order;
- a real packaged-Tesseract test covers a launcher capture, a genuinely solid frame, a nonblank unreadable gradient, and a near-solid warning frame.

Measured on the exact issue image, the retry recovered 49 words at 72% confidence (including Settings, Wallet, Tasks, Automations, and other launcher labels). Independent analysis measured 210 color buckets and a 33.7% dominant-color ratio, so the corrected verdict is `needs-eyeball`, `pixelBlank=false`, `regression=false`—never fabricated `verified`.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking).

# Documentation changes needed?

The OCR triage documentation is updated with the measured cause, retry configuration, confidence floor, retained diagnostics, and exact reproduction result.

# Risks

Medium. This changes top-level GitHub Actions concurrency identities for the ten workflows reused by the exhaustive develop orchestrator and for the canonical `test.yml` push signal. A malformed expression could cancel or queue the wrong work. Exact-expression mutation tests, the real manifest proof, and hosted overlap canaries guard that boundary.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Gives every reusable lane a caller-owned `concurrency_scope`, with `standalone` as the direct-trigger namespace and `develop-exhaustive` as the orchestrator namespace.
- Makes consecutive exhaustive runs non-cancelling and retains the full pending queue with `queue: max`.
- Keeps obsolete `test.yml` develop pushes supersedable while isolating scheduled/manual diagnostics by run ID, leaving one fail-closed aggregate result at a quiet tip.
- Extends the committed matrix proof and its mutation suite so dropped lanes, scope collisions, cancellation regressions, missing exhaustive queueing, per-run push groups, and permissive aggregate results fail closed.
- Preserves the ten-family runtime aggregate: any reusable result other than `success` remains red.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking CI orchestration correction).

# Documentation changes needed?

No public product documentation change is needed. The committed CI manifest commentary and workflow rationale document the operational contract at its source.

25:T2654,Closes #16187

## Problem

Root `test:cloud` invoked `packages/scripts/test-cloud-run.mjs` with no build prerequisite, so after a deterministic clean install (`ELIZA_SKIP_ARTIFACT_SYNC=1 bun install --frozen-lockfile`) the batches started against a tree with no `@elizaos/core` dist and no generated i18n keyword modules. Reproduced on this exact worktree at the merge-base (`ab1f1bd870c`), unmodified runner: **exit 1, 10 of 11 batches red, 669 failed tests + 424 errors**, all module-resolution cascades that read like database regressions.

The actual failure chain has three shapes (counts from the before log):

| Shape | Cause | Occurrences |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `Cannot find module '@elizaos/core' from plugins/plugin-sql/src/index.ts` | `@elizaos/core` resolves via its `bun` export condition to `packages/core/dist/node/index.node.js` — absent before a build | 142 |
| `Cannot find module './generated/validation-keyword-data.ts' from packages/core/src/i18n/…` | generated keyword module missing (core's prebuild emits it only on a real, uncached build) | 349 |
| `Cannot find module './generated/validation-keyword-data.js' from packages/shared/src/i18n/keyword-matching.ts` | generated keyword module missing in `@elizaos/shared` | 1 |

## Fix

A clean-install preflight inside `test-cloud-run.mjs` (`ensureCloudTestRuntime`), run by `main()` before any batch:

- checks the exact artifacts the cloud lane's import graph resolves: the three generated keyword modules and `packages/core/dist/node/index.node.js`;
- if keyword modules are missing → runs `packages/shared/scripts/generate-keywords.mjs` (the same script core's prebuild and shared's `build:i18n` use);
- if the core dist is missing → runs `packages/scripts/build-core.mjs` (the root `build:core` — the exact prerequisite `test:server`/`test:client`/`test:plugins` and the cloud-tests workflow's `cloud-setup-test-env` action already use: its `build:linked-workspaces` is literally `bun run --cwd ../../.. build:core`);
- re-checks after each step and **fails loudly** (step name + exit code/signal, no batches started) if a step fails or completes without producing its artifacts.

Placing the preflight in the runner (instead of a `build:core && …` prefix in package.json) keeps `windows-ci.yml`'s direct `node packages/scripts/test-cloud-run.mjs` invocation covered, costs a fully built tree only four `existsSync` calls, and repairs the turbo-cache-hit shape: the `build` task's `outputs` cache `dist/**` only, so a cache hit can restore core's dist without ever running the keyword codegen — a bare `build:core &&` prerequisite would not fix that state. The keyword-generation step is now performed by the command itself rather than being tribal knowledge (issue acceptance criterion).

PGlite readiness assertions and the runner's existing fail-closed guards are untouched.

## Tests

- **`packages/scripts/test-cloud-run-preflight.self-test.mjs`** (new; wired as `test:cloud:preflight:self-test` and as a step in `cloud-tests.yml` before `bun run test:cloud`): executes the preflight logic against simulated states — clean install (asserts codegen runs before build:core and each group is satisfied), turbo-cache-hit-without-codegen (asserts only codegen runs), dist-only-missing, fully built (asserts zero side effects); asserts the loud-failure contract (step exit code / signal / spawn error / step-succeeded-but-artifacts-still-missing / artifact group without a step); and **runs the real keyword codegen** against the repo to prove the preflight's artifact list matches what the script actually emits (path-drift guard, not a re-read of the wiring string).
- **`packages/scripts/__tests__/test-cloud-run.test.mjs`**: 9 new bun:test cases covering `ensureCloudTestRuntime` and `runPreflightStep` (25 pass total in the file).

# Risks

Medium. These scripts assemble publish output for many workspaces. The main risks are exposing a duplicate canonical package identity that traversal order previously hid, or excluding generated Python state a package accidentally depended on. Representative agent, app-core, and UI outputs were therefore built, packed, and inspected directly; ordinary Python sources remain present.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

It prevents local non-canonical state from contaminating package output:

- `prepare-package-dist` now resolves dependency versions through the repository's shared canonical workspace-discovery seam. Nested tool manifests cannot impersonate a workspace root, workspace negations remain authoritative, malformed selected manifests fail, and duplicate canonical names are rejected.
- `copy-package-assets` excludes Python bytecode, cache/type/lint/test state, virtual environments, coverage residue, and `*.egg-info` metadata while retaining source assets.

The CLI boundary still emits a non-zero build failure rather than a partial manifest. No package manifest, lockfile, workflow, runtime, UI, or generated packaging-runtime exclusion changed.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking build and publishing hardening).

# Documentation changes needed?

N/A - no user-facing command or configuration contract changed; the behavior is internal package assembly and is covered by executable tests.

## Problem

`packages/tools/voice-evidence-harness/src/cli.ts` derived both the evidence root and the Deepgram/Cartesia secret paths from `node:os.homedir()` inline, and `cli-run.test.ts` only reassigned `process.env.HOME` — which Bun's `homedir()` ignores — so every test run wrote evidence under the **real** `~/.moltbot` tree. Reproduced on this machine before the fix: running the develop-side `cli-run.test.ts` leaked `~/.moltbot/projects/eliza-fleet/evidence/voice-e2e/2026-07-13T20-11-15-312Z/` (108-byte fake `input.wav`, 4-byte fake `walkthrough.mp4`) into the real home (see linked evidence). The leaked dirs were removed after capture, mirroring the issue's remediation.

## Change

- **`src/paths.ts` (new)** — single injectable derivation seam. `VOICE_EVIDENCE_ROOT` / `VOICE_EVIDENCE_SECRETS_DIR` env overrides win; a set-but-blank or relative override **throws** (a malformed override must never silently fall back to the real home); defaults preserve the exact live `~/.moltbot` layout and evidence naming. Runtime-neutral by design (only `node:os`/`node:path`, erasable TS) so the same file executes under Bun and Node type stripping.
- **`src/cli.ts`** — evidence root + secret paths now come from the seam; no other behavior change.
- **`src/cli-run.test.ts`** — rewritten to run against the **real filesystem** (the `node:fs` `mock.module` is gone): real committed fixture WAV, real secret JSONs read from the temp secrets dir through the seam. Asserts on the **pass path** (in-process baseline run) that the full DoD artifact set lands below the `mkdtemp` root, and on the **failure path** (a spawned, fully **unmocked** `bun src/cli.ts` subprocess failing loudly on a missing `OPENROUTER_API_KEY`) that the partial evidence tree also stays below the override root. Every test snapshots the real `~/.moltbot` tree (names, kinds, sizes, mtimes) before, and asserts **zero delta** after — in the test bodies and again in `afterEach` as a safety net.
- **`src/paths.test.ts` (new)** — derivation contract under **both runtimes**: Bun leg via direct imports (override wins without consulting the home lookup, defaults on the `~/.moltbot` layout incl. the live `homedir()` default, blank/relative overrides throw), plus a **Node** leg that runs the identical assertions in a type-stripped `node` subprocess and compares observations, so a Bun-vs-Node divergence (the #16180 failure mode) surfaces as a concrete value diff.
- **`README.md`** — documents the two overrides.

## Acceptance criteria mapping

| criterion | where |
| --- | --- |
| explicit injectable evidence root + test-safe secret seam | `src/paths.ts` (`VOICE_EVIDENCE_ROOT`, `VOICE_EVIDENCE_SECRETS_DIR`, injectable env/home params) |
| preserve current live default under the real user home | `DEFAULT_EVIDENCE_SUBPATH` / `DEFAULT_SECRETS_SUBPATH`; asserted in `paths.test.ts` ("default stays on the ~/.moltbot layout", incl. real `homedir()` leg) |
| every artifact below the test mkdtemp root on success and failure | `cli-run.test.ts` — pass path walks the produced tree and checks the full DoD artifact set + `startsWith(tmpRoot)`; failure path via real unmocked CLI subprocess |
| real `~/.moltbot` has no delta after the test | `snapshotTree()` before/after (names, kinds, sizes, mtimes) in both tests + `afterEach` safety net |
| exercised under both Node and Bun | `paths.test.ts` Node subprocess leg (type-stripped import of the same module) + all Bun legs; verified on node v25.2.1 locally, CI runs node 24.15 (type stripping default since 23.6) |
| live-provider behavior and evidence naming unchanged outside tests | overrides only read from env; defaults byte-identical; run-dir naming (`<stamp>[-real-server]/<scenario>`) untouched |

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)28:T1b5d,Fixes #16230

## Root cause

The GUI chat SSE route (`POST /api/conversations/:id/messages/stream`) streams a turn's model tokens through a single ambient streaming context — `onStreamChunk` on an AsyncLocalStorage `StreamingContext` (`packages/core/src/streaming-context.ts`). Every `runtime.useModel` call made anywhere inside `processMessage` reads that context and streams into the visible reply channel (`packages/core/src/runtime.ts:5573-5606`).

Only the **top-level** reply generation should reach that channel. The visible reply is produced by the Stage-1 `RESPONSE_HANDLER` call (`packages/core/src/services/message.ts:6628`, `streamStructured: true`), which runs *before* actions. An action delivers its own output through the `HandlerCallback` — not through raw model streaming. But an action's **internal** `useModel` calls run inside the same ambient context, so their intermediate output streamed straight into the visible reply.

`/compact` is the reported trigger: the deterministic command layer fires `COMPACT_CONVERSATION`, whose compactor makes an internal `TEXT_LARGE` call (`buildActionCompactorModelCall`, `packages/agent/src/actions/compact-conversation.ts:95` — plain `runtime.useModel("TEXT_LARGE", { system, prompt })`, no explicit `onStreamChunk`). That call's raw rendered-ledger JSON streamed as the visible reply and **claimed the stream source first**, so the action's actual summary ("Compacted N older message(s); preserved the latest M.") — delivered later via the callback — was discarded (`generateChatResponse` only chunks the resolved reply `if (!streamedText && resolvedText)`, `packages/agent/src/api/chat-routes.ts:2743`). Any action making internal model calls on the GUI stream path had the same exposure.

## The fix (channel scoping, not text filtering)

New `runWithSuppressedModelStream(fn)` in `streaming-context.ts`: runs `fn` with the ambient `onStreamChunk` detached (replaced with a no-op) while **keeping the abort signal and structured tool/evaluation hooks intact**. This is the same suppression the planner loop and evaluator already apply to their own model calls (`planner-loop.ts:1621`, `evaluator.ts:117`) — now generalized for action handlers.

It is wired around **every** action-handler invocation, so the fix is generic — it excludes *any* action's internal model calls, not just compact:

- `runtime/execute-planned-tool-call.ts:406` — the planned-tool-call executor (all planner-dispatched actions)
- `runtime.ts:3701` — `runActionsByMode` (`ALWAYS_*` / `CONTEXT_*` hook + evaluator actions)
- `services/message.ts:6242` — the shortcut/command path (the `/compact` repro)

Nested sub-action handlers inherit the suppression via AsyncLocalStorage, so a single wrap at each top-level dispatch site covers the whole call tree. The top-level `RESPONSE_HANDLER` reply generation is untouched — it runs outside any action seam, so the user-visible reply still streams token-by-token. An action that genuinely wants to stream can still opt in with an explicit `onStreamChunk` in its `useModel` params, which `useModel` honors independently of the ambient context.

## How to verify

1. In a conversation with enough history, send `/compact` through the GUI chat.
2. **Before:** the visible streamed reply was the compactor's internal ```json { "state": { "facts": [...] } }``` ledger.
3. **After:** the visible reply is the designed summary ("Compacted N older message(s); preserved the latest M."); the internal ledger JSON never appears on the wire.

## Tests

New `packages/core/src/runtime/__tests__/action-streaming-suppression.test.ts` drives the **real** `executePlannedToolCall` seam (no mock of the code under test): an action whose stubbed `TEXT_LARGE` model streams intermediate ledger JSON into the active streaming context, with a real chat-SSE sink attached. Asserts the sink never receives the internal tokens while the `HandlerCallback` delivers the clean reply. Positive controls prove the negative assertions are non-vacuous (the same emission DOES stream when made at the top level) and that the abort signal + structured hooks survive the suppression.

29:T115f,Closes #16298.

## Outcome

Cerebras can spend a capped response budget on hidden reasoning and return no visible text. This correction sends `reasoning_effort` only for the two current Cerebras models whose documented contract supports it:

- `gpt-oss-120b`: defaults to `low`; `eliza.thinking="off"` also resolves to `low`, because Cerebras does not accept `none` for this model.
- `zai-glm-4.7`: defaults to `low`; `eliza.thinking="off"` resolves to `none`, so planner/formatting calls consume zero hidden reasoning tokens.

Provider-prefixed forms (`cerebras:`, `cerebras/`, and `openai/`) normalize to the exact bare id. Family substrings and undocumented lookalikes—including generic GLM/Gemma names, `gpt-oss-20b`, and `zai-glm-4.6`—receive no inferred wire field. An explicit valid caller option remains authoritative.

The native result now preserves `usage.reasoningTokens`, which makes the suppression result observable to callers and to the credentialed live receipt. The exact supported model names and suppression values follow the current [Cerebras reasoning documentation](https://inference-docs.cerebras.ai/capabilities/reasoning).

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)2b:T8fb,## What

The warm Claude Agent SDK brain (`ELIZA_CHAT_VIA_CLI=claude-sdk`) ran at the SDK's default reasoning effort (`high`) with no operator control. The Agent SDK exposes an `effort` option (`low|medium|high|xhigh|max` — the same knob as `claude --effort`); this wires it through so reasoning depth can be traded against latency/cost per tier.

- **`ELIZA_CLI_CLAUDE_EFFORT`** — effort for the reply/large tiers (RESPONSE_HANDLER / TEXT_LARGE / TEXT_MEGA).
- **`ELIZA_CLI_CLAUDE_PLANNER_EFFORT`** — effort for the ROUTE-mode planner tier independently (so routing depth tunes separately from reply depth); falls back to the shared knob, then the SDK default.
- **`normalizeEffort`** validates against the SDK's level set and **drops** an unknown value (an unrecognized level fails the whole turn), so a typo degrades to the default rather than breaking the brain.
- Forwarded as query option `effort`; **omitted entirely when unset** so the SDK keeps its own default (no behavior change for existing deployments).

# Risks

Medium operational risk, bounded by pinned images and the live monitor. Whisper's multilingual model is now part of the immutable image, which increases its image size but prevents Railway's generic `/health` endpoint from accepting a container whose required model is absent. Kokoro remains on the pinned CPU image and exposes the existing cloud contract through a thin typed adapter.

The workflow also contains separate self-hosted acoustic jobs. Their missing GPU/voice provisioning is tracked by #9454 and does not weaken or gate the GitHub-hosted Railway contract job proved here.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Binds both upstream servers to Railway's runtime-assigned `PORT`, so the process, health probe, and public domain use the same socket.
- Preserves the cloud API's `POST /api/tts { text, voice, speed } -> audio/wav` contract over Kokoro-FastAPI v0.2.2's OpenAI endpoint.
- Uses Kokoro's working streaming WAV path; its pinned complete-audio path omits the required voice while chunking and returns HTTP 500.
- Installs `Systran/faster-whisper-small` into the Speaches image during build. Speaches 0.8.2 ignores the old `WHISPER__MODEL` preload assumption and otherwise returns 404 after a healthy deploy.
- Documents the monorepo-safe `railway up . --path-as-root` owner command.
- Expands mutation-sensitive service-definition tests for runtime ports, the compatibility route, baked model, pinned images, deploy docs, and monitor wiring.

## Root-cause progression proved against real Railway

1. Fresh `develop` deployments `46857e2f-3a65-4db4-8b00-03dccbae21be` (Kokoro) and `bbcc82cc-79d7-4e89-8ccf-dd01e0783077` (Whisper) failed Railway health because their inherited launchers listened on 8880/8000 instead of injected `PORT=8080`.
2. After repairing the socket, the real cloud route exposed Kokoro's missing `/api/tts` compatibility endpoint and then the pinned upstream non-streaming voice bug. The adapter now routes through the live-proved streaming WAV path.
3. The first real round trip then exposed Whisper's empty model registry: `/health` returned 200 while transcription returned `404 Model ... is not installed locally`. The build now installs the exact model before Railway can accept the image.

Closes the `/v1/messages` gap in the #16081 reservation-parity invariant series (fixed for `/v1/chat/completions` in #16099, and the A2A / MCP agent routes in #16147 / #16148). Found while auditing cloud call sites that reserve credits and then call the provider.
